		<item>
		<title>Accelerating M&amp;A due diligence with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/accelerating-ma-due-diligence-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Anand Komandooru]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 13 Aug 2026 15:52:44 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">a1299d1fe5dce83f7bfea5a02c0c90a01c4c8284</guid>

					<description>Learn how to build a multi-agent M&amp;amp;A due diligence system on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. This post walks through a reference architecture that combines agent orchestration, knowledge retrieval, and governance controls, then deploys a complete sample you can run in your own AWS account.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Mergers and acquisitions (M&amp;amp;A) teams face a persistent challenge: conducting thorough due diligence on multiple acquisition targets while maintaining speed and analytical rigor. Teams often spend weeks manually reviewing targets before identifying viable opportunities. Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. It can accelerate this process by orchestrating AI agents that handle data gathering, analysis, and compliance checks autonomously within defined guardrails.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we show how to build a multi-agent due diligence system on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. We present a reference architecture that combines agent orchestration, knowledge retrieval, and governance controls, then walk through deploying and running the solution using a complete sample repository.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="the-ma-due-diligence-challenge"&gt;The M&amp;amp;A due diligence challenge&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In transportation and logistics, for example, analysts pull data from financial databases, market research applications, regulatory filings, and internal knowledge bases, then reconcile that information by hand. The process is slow and resource intensive.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The problem compounds when teams duplicate work. Industry research, valuation models, and competitive analyses get recreated for each new deal instead of building on institutional knowledge from prior transactions. Meanwhile, governance concerns slow AI adoption because legal and compliance teams require confidence that AI-generated insights are accurate, traceable, and supported by source citations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These four pressures (slow cycles, fragmented data, duplicated effort, and governance requirements) create an opportunity for AI agents to fundamentally change how due diligence operates.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-ai-agents-transform-due-diligence"&gt;How AI agents transform due diligence&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AI agents address each of these challenges through a combination of autonomous data gathering, intelligent routing, persistent memory, and built-in auditability.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On the data-gathering front, agents autonomously query multiple sources (financial databases, knowledge bases, market data APIs) and synthesize the results into preliminary assessments. In our testing, work that previously required weeks of analyst time was completed in hours because the agent handles the repetitive search-and-summarize loop without human intervention.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For prioritization, agents evaluate opportunities against strategic criteria and route high-priority deals to the right specialists. Rather than reviewing every target sequentially, the system surfaces the most promising candidates first and flags the specific dimensions (financial performance, strategic fitness, compliance risk) that warrant deeper analysis.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Agents also build institutional knowledge over time. Each completed analysis enriches a shared memory layer so that future deals benefit from prior research, valuation assumptions, and integration lessons. Teams stop recreating context from scratch.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Governance is built into the agent workflow from the start, not added as an afterthought. The system is configured so that each assertion the agent emits is grounded in a citation, validated by the citation-check evaluator. Each agent invocation produces an audit trail, and guardrails enforce organizational policies at runtime. Compliance teams can trace each cited claim back to its source document.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="architecture-decision-integrated-suite-or-custom-build"&gt;Architecture decision: integrated suite or custom build&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We present two implementation paths, each suited to different organizational needs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="option-1-amazon-quick-integrated-solution"&gt;Option 1: Amazon Quick (integrated solution)&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick provides ready-to-use AI capabilities tailored for business intelligence and research workflows. It offers a fast path to production if your needs align with standard analysis patterns. Amazon Quick Research generates professional, exportable in-depth reports. Amazon Quick Flows automates repetitive tasks with pre-built workflows. Amazon Quick Index delivers unified search across connected data sources.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose Amazon Quick if you want to deploy rapidly, your workflows align with standard business intelligence patterns, and you prefer fully managed services over custom development. For additional information and reference architecture, see the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quicksight/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Announcing Amazon Quick&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can also extend Quick with custom Amazon Bedrock AgentCore agents for specialized requirements such as proprietary valuation models or strategic fit frameworks. For a practical example of this pattern, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-an-intelligent-contract-management-solution-with-amazon-quick-suite-and-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Building Intelligent Contract Management with Quick and AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;. That post demonstrates how Amazon Quick handles document management and the user interface while Amazon Bedrock AgentCore powers specialized agent collaboration.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="option-2-custom-architecture-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore"&gt;Option 2: Custom architecture with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; gives you fine-grained control over agent behavior, memory, and coordination. This approach suits M&amp;amp;A teams with proprietary methodologies, complex multi-agent coordination requirements, integration with specialized internal systems, or a need for complete control over agent behavior and model selection.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The remainder of this post explores this custom architecture in detail using a transportation-and-logistics due diligence scenario as context. We deploy a working multi-agent system, invoke it against synthetic targets, and validate outputs through an automated citation-check evaluator.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="reference-architecture"&gt;Reference architecture&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For M&amp;amp;A teams choosing the custom path, the following reference architecture shows how the pieces fit together end to end.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/07/ML-19621-1.png" alt="Reference architecture of a supervisor agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore coordinating four specialist due diligence agents" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 1: Multi-agent M&amp;amp;A due diligence architecture with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture uses Amazon Bedrock AgentCore to orchestrate a multi-agent due diligence workflow. A supervisor agent coordinates four specialist agents, each responsible for a distinct phase of the due diligence process. Users interact through a Jupyter notebook or terminal interface, while AgentCore runtime manages agent execution and collaboration. The system combines Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG), structured financial analysis, external tool integration, memory, and policy-based governance to support production-ready due diligence workflows.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="agent-orchestration-layer"&gt;Agent orchestration layer&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime coordinates multiple specialized agents through the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt;. The supervisor agent uses the agents-as-tools pattern to route requests to domain-specific specialists based on task requirements.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Target Screening Agent&lt;/strong&gt; identifies acquisition candidates by converting natural-language queries into SQL and executing them against &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/CHAP_AuroraOverview.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Aurora&lt;/a&gt; PostgreSQL. When analysts ask for “mid-market logistics companies with revenue between 100M and 500M USD and EBITDA margins above 12 percent,” the agent translates that into a parameterized query. It then retrieves matching rows and enriches the results with narrative context from the knowledge base.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Financial Analysis Agent&lt;/strong&gt; performs valuation analysis using structured and unstructured data sources. It applies standard valuation methodologies including discounted cash flow (DCF) analysis and comparable company analysis, pulling market multiples through an AgentCore Gateway-backed tool. The agent generates preliminary valuations with supporting assumptions documented inline and flags management projections that diverge from historical performance.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Strategic Fit Agent&lt;/strong&gt; evaluates integration risks, synergies, and organizational alignment. It retrieves context from prior transactions stored in AgentCore memory (using a dedicated &lt;code&gt;prior_deals&lt;/code&gt; namespace). It then compares the current target’s profile against completed acquisitions and identifies integration risks with citations to source memos.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;strong&gt;Compliance Validation Agent&lt;/strong&gt; audits responses against the M&amp;amp;A governance checklist. It invokes a custom citation-check evaluator (implemented as an AWS Lambda function) that examines every factual claim in a response and flags assertions that lack a supporting source citation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="key-architecture-components"&gt;Key architecture components&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture combines structured and unstructured enterprise data to ground agent responses in verified sources.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/CHAP_AuroraOverview.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Aurora&lt;/a&gt; PostgreSQL Serverless v2 stores structured financial and operational datasets. The Target Screening Agent generates SQL from natural language and executes queries through the RDS Data API, providing fast access to target-company metrics without requiring analysts to write SQL themselves.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/knowledge-base.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases&lt;/a&gt;, the fully managed RAG capability in Amazon Bedrock, indexes due diligence documents including confidential information memoranda (CIMs), financial statements, press packs, and internal governance checklists. Agents retrieve relevant passages and cite them inline.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/strong&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, lets agents securely interact with external tools and services through Model Context Protocol (MCP)-compatible integrations. In this architecture, agents invoke an &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/welcome.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt;-based market data service through the gateway, with Cedar policies enforcing deterministic access controls over tool usage and agent interactions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore memory&lt;/strong&gt; maintains contextual state and reusable institutional knowledge across interactions. Session memory preserves conversation continuity, while a long-term &lt;code&gt;prior_deals&lt;/code&gt; namespace stores lessons from completed acquisitions that inform future analyses.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore Evaluations&lt;/strong&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, uses Lambda-based evaluators to automatically validate output quality, citation accuracy, and domain-specific criteria. The citation-check evaluator returns a structured pass/fail result with counts of supported and unsupported claims.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/guardrails.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; enforces safety and response controls throughout the workflow, applied at the supervisor level so specialist outputs pass through organizational policy checks.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every invocation produces an &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/WhatIsCloudWatch.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudWatch&lt;/a&gt; log stream and an &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/xray/latest/devguide/aws-xray.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS X-Ray&lt;/a&gt; trace that captures the full supervisor-to-specialist-to-tool call hierarchy, providing end-to-end observability.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="security"&gt;Security&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;M&amp;amp;A due diligence involves some of the most sensitive data a company handles: unreleased financials, deal terms, and integration plans. Security is therefore a first-class part of the architecture. Every IAM permission the agents can invoke is scoped to a specific Amazon Resource Name (ARN) rather than a wildcard. The AgentCore Gateway market-data tool goes a step further with a Cedar policy engine that enforces default-deny, attribute-based authorization. The tool runs only when the requested industry code matches an approved vertical. Everything else is denied before it runs. The database tier lives in private, internet-isolated subnets behind virtual private cloud (VPC) endpoints. Data stores are encrypted at rest with KMS, and Amazon Bedrock Guardrails screen every supervisor response for harmful content and personalized financial advice. This layered model of IAM, Cedar, network isolation, and encryption covers different failure modes for systems that handle confidential deal data.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="deploy-and-run-the-sample"&gt;Deploy and run the sample&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The complete reference implementation is available in the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;M&amp;amp;A Due Diligence Multi-Agent Sample&lt;/a&gt; repository. It ships with synthetic data (no real companies, financial data, or personally identifiable information), one-command deployment, and a Jupyter notebook walkthrough. The estimated cost for a full deploy-run-cleanup cycle is under USD $5.00.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before deploying, verify you have:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with Amazon Bedrock model access enabled for Anthropic Claude and Amazon Nova. For model availability by AWS Region, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI) v2.15 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.11 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Node.js 20 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit (AWS CDK) v2 (&lt;code&gt;npm install -g aws-cdk&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;You don’t need Docker installed locally. The agent container image is built on AWS CodeBuild.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deploy to one of the supported Regions: &lt;code&gt;us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;us-west-2&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;ap-southeast-2&lt;/code&gt;, or &lt;code&gt;eu-central-1&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-1-deploy-the-stack"&gt;Step 1: Deploy the stack&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Clone the repository and run the deploy script. First-time deployment takes 20–25 minutes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# macOS / Linux
git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore.git
cd sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore
./deploy.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-powershell"&gt;# Windows
git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore.git
cd sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore
.\deploy.ps1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The deploy script verifies your Region and model access, creates a virtual environment, runs &lt;code&gt;cdk deploy --all&lt;/code&gt;, seeds synthetic data, and executes a post-deploy smoke test. It also installs the project in editable mode, which registers the &lt;code&gt;mna&lt;/code&gt; command-line tool that you use to invoke agents in the next step.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-2-invoke-the-agents"&gt;Step 2: Invoke the agents&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Use the mna CLI to send prompts to individual specialists or let the supervisor route across them:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# Let the supervisor orchestrate across specialists
mna invoke supervisor "Screen the mid-market logistics targets with revenue 100M-500M, then run a DCF on the top hit." --session-id walkthrough-session-00000000-0001

# Target Screening: text-to-SQL on Aurora + KB narrative enrichment
mna invoke target_screening "Screen the target pipeline for transportation companies with revenue between 100M and 500M USD, EBITDA margin above 12%, and fleet size above 200. Surface the top three and tell me what the CIM says about the leader's growth trajectory." --session-id walkthrough-session-00000000-0001

# Financial Analysis: KB retrieval + Gateway-backed market data tool
mna invoke financial_analysis "Run a DCF on Example Corp using the CIM in the knowledge base. Flag any management projection that diverges from historical performance by more than 20% and pull comparable multiples for transportation-logistics mid-market." --session-id walkthrough-session-00000000-0001

# Strategic Fit: AgentCore Memory long-term retrieval
mna invoke strategic_fit "Compare Example Corp' integration profile against our three most recent completed acquisitions. Identify the top three integration risks and cite the source memos." --session-id walkthrough-session-00000000-0001&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every invocation prints a &lt;code&gt;trace_id&lt;/code&gt; in its footer. Use &lt;code&gt;mna trace &amp;lt;trace_id&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; to inspect the full X-Ray trace for that call.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-3-validate-outputs-with-the-evaluator"&gt;Step 3: Validate outputs with the evaluator&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The mna evaluate subcommand runs the citation-check evaluator against a specialist’s output:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# Capture a response in JSON format
mna --json invoke financial_analysis "Run a DCF on Example Corp using the CIM." --session-id blog-demo-session-00000000-000002 &amp;gt; run.json

# --session-id accepts any string between 33 and 256 characters --- AgentCore Runtime enforces this length range for runtimeSessionId. The value above is just an example; use a unique string per session (a UUID works well) if you want to keep invocations isolated or omit --session-id entirely to let the client generate one for you.

# Extract response text and citations
python -c "import json,pathlib;d=json.loads(pathlib.Path('run.json').read_text());pathlib.Path('response.txt').write_text(d['text'])"

python -c "import json,pathlib;
d=json.loads(pathlib.Path('run.json').read_text());
pathlib.Path('citations.json').write_text(json.dumps(d['citations']))"

# Run the citation-check evaluator
mna evaluate --response-file response.txt --citations-file citations.json&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The evaluator returns a structured result indicating pass or failure, with counts of total claims, supported claims, and any unsupported assertions. The exit code is 0 on pass and 1 on fail, making it composable in CI pipelines.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-4-explore-the-notebook"&gt;Step 4: Explore the notebook&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For a guided, cell-by-cell tour of the same workflow:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;jupyter lab notebooks/walkthrough.ipynb&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The notebook walks through environment validation, data overview, one cell per specialist agent (with rendered responses and citations), and trace inspection.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, destroy all deployed resources as soon as you finish exploring:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# macOS / Linux
./cleanup.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-powershell"&gt;# Windows
.\cleanup.ps1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The cleanup script runs &lt;code&gt;cdk destroy --all --force&lt;/code&gt; to tear down every stack in reverse dependency order. It then executes a verification sweep that scans for orphaned resources:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;S3 buckets matching &lt;code&gt;mna-*&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;ECR repositories matching mna-*.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases with &lt;code&gt;mna&lt;/code&gt; in the name.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AgentCore runtimes, memories, and gateways.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AWS CloudFormation stacks starting with &lt;code&gt;Mna&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The verification script prints copy-paste commands for resources it finds so you can confirm a zero-cost end state. If the script output is empty for every sweep, all billable resources have been removed. For additional details on CDK resource cleanup, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cdk/v2/guide/ref-cli-cmd-destroy.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CDK documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="next-steps"&gt;Next steps&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We encourage you to extend this architecture for your own use cases. Here are several paths forward:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Add a production front end.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/accelerate-agentic-application-development-with-a-full-stack-starter-template-for-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Full-Stack Starter Template for AgentCore (FAST)&lt;/a&gt; provides a React-based UI, Amazon Cognito authentication, and Amazon CloudFront distribution that integrates directly with AgentCore runtime.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Build additional specialist agents.&lt;/strong&gt; The sample’s CONTRIBUTING.md documents the file layout, Strands pattern, and supervisor registration process for adding new agents (for example, a regulatory-filing agent or an ESG-scoring agent).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Explore advanced orchestration.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt; supports multiple coordination approaches including agents-as-tools, swarms, and agent graphs. The &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/agentic-ai-patterns/multi-agent-collaboration.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;multi-agent collaboration patterns documentation&lt;/a&gt; covers each approach in detail.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Connect additional data sources.&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore Gateway supports HTTP APIs, MCP servers, and Amazon API Gateway targets beyond the single Lambda tool demonstrated here.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we showed how to build a multi-agent system on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that automates M&amp;amp;A due diligence in transportation and logistics. We walked through the architecture that coordinates specialized agents for target screening, financial analysis, strategic fit assessment, and compliance validation. We then deployed the working sample and invoked it against synthetic acquisition targets.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The reference implementation demonstrates how each architectural layer (agent orchestration, knowledge retrieval, structured data access, institutional memory, and automated evaluation) contributes to a system that is both capable and auditable. The patterns shown here extend naturally to other due diligence domains including healthcare, financial services, and technology acquisitions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, clone the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;sample repository&lt;/a&gt;, deploy the stack in your AWS account, and try modifying a specialist agent or adding a new one. The FAST template provides the path from this sample to a hosted application with authentication, observability, and a polished UI.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="resources"&gt;Resources&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-ma-due-diligence-agentcore" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;M&amp;amp;A Due Diligence Multi-Agent Sample&lt;/a&gt; – GitHub repository&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt;

		<title>Amazon Quick for Microsoft 365: Agentic AI where you work</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-quick-for-microsoft-365-agentic-ai-where-you-work/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Art Chan]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 13 Aug 2026 15:48:15 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Quick Suite]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">f7e47af42f400e3f4aeaea3b8fcc2ab80d496253</guid>

					<description>Amazon Quick is now available directly inside Microsoft Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook. These extensions bring connected data access and agentic document editing into the Microsoft 365 apps your teams already use, so you can analyze data, draft content, and reach enterprise knowledge without switching applications.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Your enterprise data lives in dozens of systems, but often the work happens in Microsoft 365. Amazon Quick bridges that gap as an AI assistant grounded in your data, now available directly inside Microsoft Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These extensions don’t require you to adopt a new application or change how you work. Instead, they bring Amazon Quick connected data access and agentic document editing directly into the Microsoft 365 apps your teams open every morning. Your Amazon Quick Sight dashboards, Spaces, AWS data sources, and third-party integrations are now accessible without leaving Word, Excel, PowerPoint, or Outlook.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we cover how installation and setup works, and workflows that you can run across each app.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="general-availability-and-access"&gt;General availability and access&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick customers can access the new Microsoft 365 extensions now. You can install the four extensions (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook) across both desktop and web versions of Microsoft 365. No additional licensing is required for customers on Plus, Professional, or Enterprise plans.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-this-matters-the-intelligence-layer"&gt;Why this matters: The intelligence layer&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;What really matters is how you embed AI into the workflows where business decisions happen.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Most organizations also use AWS infrastructure, Amazon Quick Sight dashboards, Salesforce pipelines, Jira projects, Slack conversations, and SharePoint repositories. Quick connects these to your daily productivity tools. The value isn’t in the underlying agentic model itself. It’s in the breadth of connected data that the agent brings to your interactions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An example from my own workflow: I now draft customer request for proposal responses in hours, pulling from internal knowledge bases, past proposals, and live customer data. This used to take a team weeks to compile from scattered sources. Customized customer presentations that weave together structured data sources and unstructured documents come together in an hour rather than a day or two. The time I save goes straight into refining positioning and focusing on what matters to each customer, rather than scrambling to gather information and meet a response deadline.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-1.png" alt="Amazon Quick console with the Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook extensions activated and ready for deployment" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 1: The Amazon Quick console with Microsoft 365 extensions enabled, showing Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook extensions activated and ready for deployment&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-it-works-agentic-not-only-chat"&gt;How it works: Agentic, not only chat&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These extensions aren’t a chatbot bolted onto Office, and they aren’t a standalone application that asks you to work somewhere new. They are agentic: the AI assistant does more than answer questions. It takes action directly within your documents, spreadsheets, presentations, and email messages.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Quick lives inside Microsoft Word as a persistent side panel. You open it from the ribbon, ask a question or give an instruction, and the agent works within the application context. There’s no tab-switching and no copy-pasting between apps. The agent sees your document and acts on it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In Word, the agent can find and replace text, insert sections, and reformat content. It then shows you a visual comparison of what changed.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every agent action in a document is tracked. In Word, changes appear as an audit trail link, or as a visual comparison directly in the chat. Each change in the audit trail includes a selectable reference back to the affected content.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Conversations don’t vanish when you close the panel either. The extension preserves history across sessions. In Microsoft Outlook, each email thread gets its own persistent conversation. Switch between email threads, and the extension remembers what you discussed in each one.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-2.png" alt="Amazon Quick side panel in Word showing a before-and-after visual comparison of agent-suggested edits" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 2: The Amazon Quick side panel in Word displaying a visual comparison of agent-suggested edits with before-and-after change tracking. In this example I’ve asked it to rewrite the opening to this post, showing changes in both Word and the audit trail that can be displayed in the agent side panel&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="installation-and-authentication-fewer-moving-parts"&gt;Installation and authentication: Fewer moving parts&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The extensions require no client-side installation. They run entirely in the cloud, so there’s nothing to deploy to user machines. An administrator can push them to targeted users and groups through the Microsoft 365 admin center using a standard manifest. This is the same mechanism used for any Microsoft 365 add-in.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Alternatively, you can install it yourself through the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/download/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Microsoft add-in store&lt;/a&gt;. Search for “Quick,” choose &lt;strong&gt;Add&lt;/strong&gt;, and the extension appears on the Office ribbon. Either way, after it’s there, updates ship automatically. Your team deploys once, and every user gets the latest version without IT intervention.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The one exception is Outlook. Because most organizations restrict Graph API permissions, the Outlook extension typically requires admin approval for full functionality. Plan on admin deployment for Outlook.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The authentication model uses Quick native authentication and does not require an Entra app. The result is fewer moving parts, faster setup, and fewer dependencies for your IT team to manage, while maintaining the security framework that Quick provides. If you’re on the Free or Plus Quick plan, you can sign in with your enterprise credentials or through social login (Google, Apple), with no separate identity configuration to set up.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can configure agent settings and custom branding through the Office manifest or policy settings, with no additional infrastructure required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can access the extensions across seven Quick AWS Regions: US East (N. Virginia), US West (Oregon), Europe (Ireland), Europe (London), Europe (Frankfurt), Asia Pacific (Sydney), and Asia Pacific (Tokyo). Data stays within your selected region, and the backend infrastructure runs fully isolated with no public egress.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Getting started requires an active Quick application. The extensions connect to your configured Quick environment, inheriting your existing knowledge bases, data sources, and integrations. They’re an expansion of the Quick you already use, not a separate product to purchase or provision.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After the extension is in place, you find the Quick icon on the Office ribbon. Select it to open, sign in, and start working. The interface is already familiar because you stay inside Microsoft 365 apps. Your existing Spaces, dashboards, and integrations (including Salesforce, Jira, Slack, SharePoint, and more) are available immediately without additional configuration. The extensions respect your existing permissions and security policies.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-3.png" alt="Seven AWS Regions where Amazon Quick extensions are available, with data residency controls" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 3: Amazon Quick extensions are available across seven AWS Regions with full data residency controls&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="microsoft-excel-where-numbers-become-decisions"&gt;Microsoft Excel: Where numbers become decisions&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent operates within the context of the data you’re already working with.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://marketplace.microsoft.com/en-us/product/WA200010611?tab=Overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;The extension&lt;/a&gt; handles the data retrieval and formatting in a single step, so you go from question to answer without switching between systems.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This helps you decode complex spreadsheets built by others or quickly trace the source of calculation discrepancies.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you need to bring in external data, the extension pulls from cloud storage, data warehouses, Quick Sight, Salesforce, or SharePoint. It then identifies inconsistencies, cleans formatting issues, and transforms everything into analysis-ready formats.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can then validate your analysis against actual business metrics stored in Quick Sight to verify your models align with reality. This closed-loop workflow keeps your spreadsheets connected to live data throughout the analysis process.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-4.png" alt="Amazon Quick side panel in Excel explaining a selected cell’s formula with highlighted references across worksheets" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 4: Quick modifies cells and explains formulas directly in the side panel, with highlighted references across worksheets. In this example, a cell is selected and you can interact with the agent to understand how a formula is derived. This example is working against the web version of Excel&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Consider a typical Excel workflow. Ask the agent to analyze your current sheet and flag month-over-month anomalies greater than 10 percent. Then pull Q1 revenue by Region from your Quick Sight dashboard into a new tab. If you encounter a complex formula, ask Quick to explain it and trace its dependencies. When you’re ready to present, have the agent create a chart that compares actual and forecast values.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="microsoft-word-write-with-enterprise-context"&gt;Microsoft Word: Write with enterprise context&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://marketplace.microsoft.com/en-us/product/WA200010615?tab=Overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;The Word extension&lt;/a&gt; acts as a co-editor that understands both your document and your connected data sources. It respects your document’s existing structure, including fonts, heading styles, and organizational patterns. When it adds or modifies a section, the output matches what’s already there. The new extension works directly with the native formatting model in Word, maintaining consistent styling throughout.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The extension captures every edit as a before-and-after snapshot and renders it as a visual comparison in the chat panel. Each change includes a selectable reference back to the affected section. You can review what was modified, where, and in what order. This gives you a complete record of the agent’s work. The audit trail is especially valuable in collaborative environments where multiple people contribute to the same document and need to understand what changed and why.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Generate tables and visualizations that pull from Quick Sight dashboards. Build executive summaries that reference Salesforce pipeline metrics or include project status updates from Jira.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Rather than copying numbers from one system and pasting them into a document, the extension weaves quantitative data from multiple sources into narrative documents. The result is reports, proposals, and briefs that cite real numbers rather than placeholder text.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-5.png" alt="Agent-suggested Word edits shown as before-and-after blocks in the chat pane" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 5: Agent-suggested edits can be displayed as before and after blocks within the chat pane&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In practice, you might start with a long report and ask Quick to summarize it into a one-page executive brief with key takeaways.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Need to reach a non-technical audience? Ask Quick to rewrite a specific section while preserving the underlying data points. For strategic documents, prompt the extension to add a conclusion section that references your Salesforce pipeline metrics from last quarter. The agent queries the data and writes the narrative in one step.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="microsoft-outlook-email-informed-by-your-enterprise-data"&gt;Microsoft Outlook: Email informed by your enterprise data&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://marketplace.microsoft.com/en-us/product/WA200010695?tab=Overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;The Outlook extension&lt;/a&gt; automatically understands the context of the email or meeting you’re reviewing. Subject, sender, recipients, thread history, and timestamps are included with every interaction, so the agent’s responses stay relevant to that context. There’s no need to explain what you’re looking at or paste in background.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Composing messages becomes faster and more informed. The agent drafts replies, reply-all messages, and new email messages with full thread awareness. It understands conversation boundaries across email clients including Outlook, Gmail, and Apple Mail, and preserves the correct thread structure in its responses.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Add recipients to To, CC, or BCC fields, include attachments, and schedule sends. The side panel handles these tasks without leaving your inbox.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Beyond composition, the extension helps you stay on top of your inbox. Flag, categorize, and move messages. Summarize long threads and extract action items. The extension turns Outlook from a communication tool into an intelligent workflow surface connected to your Salesforce accounts, Quick Sight metrics, and Jira tickets. Reference any of these directly in your email responses, grounding every message in actual business context.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-6.png" alt="Amazon Quick drafting a contextual email reply in Outlook using customer data and thread history" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 6: Quick can draft contextual replies using customer data in Quick and thread history, without leaving your inbox&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When responding to a customer, prompt Quick to pull the latest account health metrics from Salesforce and weave them into your response. The result is a message grounded in real account data.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For long threads that have gone cold, ask for a digest that summarizes the conversation with owners and deadlines called out explicitly. Or, for threads with many participants and open items, prompt Quick to create a 30-minute meeting with everyone on the thread to align on next steps. The agent generates the attendees, title, and agenda automatically.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="microsoft-powerpoint-presentations-backed-by-real-data"&gt;Microsoft PowerPoint: Presentations backed by real data&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://marketplace.microsoft.com/en-us/product/WA200010606?tab=Overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;The PowerPoint extension&lt;/a&gt; generates slides programmatically and supports multi-step creative workflows that go well beyond single-prompt generation. You build presentations iteratively, refining design and content across multiple interactions with the agent.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Quick uses your slide masters, layouts, fonts, and color schemes, so generated slides match the design system your team already uses. You don’t need to choose between AI assistance and your company’s templates. The agent works within your brand guidelines from the start.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Define a theme or color palette in one step and apply it across slides in the next, building a narrative arc over several conversations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The extension also connects your presentations to your data. Generate charts and executive summaries that reference Quick Sight dashboards or Salesforce metrics. When you’ve built an analysis in Excel, the PowerPoint extension can generate a board deck that understands what the analysis found and structures the narrative accordingly. Context flows from data to presentation because both extensions draw on the same connected data sources.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/31/ML-20556-7.png" alt="Amazon Quick PowerPoint extension generating branded slides with a suggested Quick Action button" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 7: The PowerPoint extension generates branded slides with Quick data across a multi-step workflow. In this example we show the Quick Action feature, where you can choose a suggested action&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To try this, create a five-slide executive summary using your company template, pulling Q1 metrics from your Quick Sight dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="whats-next"&gt;What’s next&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This release brings Quick intelligence to Microsoft 365, including Excel and PowerPoint. Visit the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick documentation&lt;/a&gt; for new capabilities and updates as they become available.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick extensions for Microsoft 365 are generally available. If you’re already a Quick user, you can install extensions from the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/download/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quick Download page&lt;/a&gt;. If you aren’t a Quick user, you can &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;start a free trial&lt;/a&gt;, install the extensions, and start using Quick in Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To learn more, visit the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick documentation&lt;/a&gt; or sign in to the Quick console and choose the Microsoft 365 extensions from the Extensions menu.&lt;/p&gt;

		<item>
		<title>Part 2: Amazon Bedrock cost attribution with Amazon Athena and CUDOS</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/part-2-amazon-bedrock-cost-attribution-with-amazon-athena-and-cudos/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Abhi Shivaditya]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 12 Aug 2026 17:45:20 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Athena]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">bdab25a99afb30707f36430d746dc6f3704a1935</guid>

					<description>Learn how to visualize and analyze Amazon Bedrock cost attribution using Amazon Athena and CUDOS dashboards. This post shows how to set up CUR 2.0 with IAM principal data, query Bedrock spend by principal, project, and team, and build dashboards to track AI costs across your organization.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-granular-cost-attribution-for-amazon-bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Part 1&lt;/a&gt; introduced granular cost attribution for Amazon Bedrock. This feature automatically traces every inference request back to the IAM principal that made the call. It showed how the new &lt;code&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/code&gt; column can give you per-user and per-application visibility. With optional cost allocation tags, you can also aggregate spend by team, project, or tenant using AWS Cost Explorer. With this approach, you can typically track usage at the granularity you want for any Bedrock-powered service or application, whether third-party tools like Claude Code or Codex or your own custom builds. This post shows how to visualize and analyze cost attribution for Amazon Bedrock through Amazon Athena queries and CUDOS dashboards.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;First, this post shows you how to set up Cost and Usage Report (CUR) 2.0 through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cur/latest/userguide/dataexports-create.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Data Exports&lt;/a&gt; instrumented with IAM principal data. Next, this post shows you how to query CUR data with Amazon Athena for analysis. It then introduces the new capabilities of CUDOS dashboards, including granular Bedrock cost and usage data. While Athena provides flexibility for aggregations, integration with different business intelligence (BI) tools, and chargeback processes, CUDOS offers pre-built visuals tailored to your organization’s specific structure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-21078-1.png" alt="Table of example query output with columns for IAM principal, usage type, and unblended cost, showing per-user Claude model token costs" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Example query output showing Amazon Bedrock costs by IAM principal and usage type&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="setting-up-cost-and-usage-reports-cur-2.0"&gt;Setting up Cost and Usage Reports (CUR 2.0)&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you can analyze Amazon Bedrock costs, set up a CUR 2.0 data export and connect it to Amazon Athena.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You need the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with billing console access.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;IAM permissions for Cost and Usage Reports, S3, and Athena.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An S3 bucket for CUR data storage.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Basic familiarity with SQL and the AWS Management Console.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;(Optional) Claude Code or Kiro-CLI for automated setup.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Make sure you have the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-cur-iam-principal-bedrock-tracking/tree/main#required-iam-permissions" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;required IAM permissions&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For Amazon Bedrock cost attribution, you need to enable IAM principal data in your CUR 2.0 export so that the &lt;code&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/code&gt; column and associated IAM principal tags are populated.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="creating-a-cur-2.0-data-export-with-iam-principal-data"&gt;Creating a CUR 2.0 data export with IAM principal data&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Follow the instructions in &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cur/latest/userguide/dataexports-create-standard.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Creating a standard data export&lt;/a&gt; to set up your CUR 2.0 export. When configuring the export, make sure the following options are part of your final configuration:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-21078-2.png" alt="The Create export page in the AWS Billing and Cost Management console with Standard data export, CUR 2.0, and Include caller identity (IAM principal) allocation data selected" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Creating a CUR 2.0 standard data export with caller identity (IAM principal) allocation data enabled&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In &lt;strong&gt;Additional export content&lt;/strong&gt;, select the following checkboxes: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Include caller identity (IAM principal) allocation data&lt;/strong&gt; — This is the critical setting that populates the &lt;code&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/code&gt; column and surfaces IAM principal tags (prefixed with &lt;code&gt;iamPrincipal/&lt;/code&gt;) in your cost data.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In &lt;strong&gt;Data table configurations&lt;/strong&gt;: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Time granularity&lt;/strong&gt;: Select Hourly for maximum detail.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In &lt;strong&gt;Data export delivery options&lt;/strong&gt;: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;File versioning&lt;/strong&gt;: Select Overwrite existing report to avoid storing duplicate data.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Important:&lt;/strong&gt; Enabling IAM principal data increases CUR file sizes because usage that was previously a single row is now expanded into multiple rows, one for each IAM principal that contributed to the usage. For high-volume workloads with many distinct principals, plan your Amazon S3 storage accordingly and consider &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/object-lifecycle-mgmt.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3 Lifecycle policies&lt;/a&gt; for older CUR files.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;It may take up to 24 hours for AWS to deliver your first CUR 2.0 report to the S3 bucket.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="connecting-cur-2.0-to-amazon-athena"&gt;Connecting CUR 2.0 to Amazon Athena&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Amazon Athena, you can query your CUR data using standard SQL with no infrastructure to manage. To streamline this setup, an optional agent.md skill repo is available that you can use with AI assistants like Claude Code, Kiro-CLI, or Codex. It automates the end-to-end process of connecting your Athena environment to your CUR data.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Clone the repo (&lt;code&gt;git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-cur-iam-principal-bedrock-tracking&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Launch Claude Code (&lt;code&gt;claude&lt;/code&gt;) or Kiro CLI (&lt;code&gt;kiro&lt;/code&gt;) in this directory.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Prompt: “Read agent.md and follow its workflow to set up Cost and Usage Report tracking and run the Amazon Bedrock-by-principal query for the current month.”&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You may also follow the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-cur-iam-principal-bedrock-tracking/blob/main/manual_setup.md" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;manual setup steps&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you plan to deploy the CUDOS dashboard as well, you can &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/deployment-in-global-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;deploy it from AWS CloudFormation&lt;/a&gt;. This also deploys the Athena query database as part of the process.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Once deployed, here is a sample test query in the &lt;a href="https://us-east-1.console.aws.amazon.com/athena/home?region=us-east-1#/query-editor/history" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Athena Query Editor&lt;/a&gt; that you can use:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT
    line_item_iam_principal,
    line_item_usage_type,
    line_item_unblended_cost
    --# Note: replace your_cur_table_name, like `cid_data_export.cur2`
FROM your_cur_table_name
WHERE line_item_product_code in ('AmazonBedrock', 'AmazonBedrockService')
    AND line_item_iam_principal IS NOT NULL
LIMIT 10;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If this returns rows with IAM principal ARNs and Bedrock usage types, your setup is complete and ready for deeper analysis.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="athena-query-patterns-for-bedrock-cost-tracking"&gt;Athena query patterns for Bedrock cost tracking&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With your CUR 2.0 data available in Athena, you can now answer granular cost attribution questions using SQL. This section provides three progressively sophisticated query patterns that cover the most common analysis scenarios.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Note:&lt;/strong&gt; In the following queries, replace &lt;code&gt;your_cur_table_name&lt;/code&gt; with the actual name of your CUR Athena table (for example, &lt;code&gt;cid_data_export.cur2&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="query-1-bedrock-costs-by-iam-principal-and-usage-type"&gt;Query 1: Bedrock costs by IAM principal and usage type&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This query provides a complete breakdown of Amazon Bedrock spending by caller identity and model usage. It answers the question: &lt;em&gt;“Who is calling which models, and how much are they spending?”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT
    line_item_iam_principal,
    line_item_usage_type,
    SUM(line_item_usage_amount) AS total_tokens,
    SUM(line_item_unblended_cost) AS total_cost
FROM your_cur_table_name
WHERE line_item_product_code in ('AmazonBedrock', 'AmazonBedrockService')
    AND billing_period = DATE_FORMAT(CURRENT_DATE, '%Y-%m')
    AND line_item_iam_principal IS NOT NULL
    -- AND line_item_usage_type LIKE '%Sonnet%input%'
GROUP BY
    line_item_iam_principal,
    line_item_usage_type
ORDER BY total_cost DESC;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Example output:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;line_item_usage_type&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_tokens&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_cost&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/ChatApp/session-1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-anthropic.claude-opus-4-8-mantle-cache-write-tokens-standard&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1629.5&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$11.2029&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/DocProcessor/batch-7&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.6Sonnet-output-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;68.579&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$1.131&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/ClaudeCode/chat&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.6Sonnet-cache-write-input-token-count&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;831.74&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$3.4309&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:iam::123456789012:user/alice&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.6Sonnet-input-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;17.33&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$0.0572&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Analysis tips:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Use LIKE patterns such as &lt;code&gt;line_item_usage_type LIKE '%Sonnet%output%'&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;%nova%&lt;/code&gt; to filter for specific models.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The &lt;code&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/code&gt; column contains the full ARN. For assumed roles, the session name after the last &lt;code&gt;/&lt;/code&gt; identifies the specific user or session.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="query-2-costs-broken-down-by-known-iam-principal-tags"&gt;Query 2: Costs broken down by known IAM principal tags&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you’ve tagged your IAM principals with dimensions like &lt;code&gt;team&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;project&lt;/code&gt;, or &lt;code&gt;costcenter&lt;/code&gt; (and activated those tags as cost allocation tags), they appear in your CUR 2.0 data as part of the &lt;em&gt;tags&lt;/em&gt; columns, with the &lt;code&gt;iamPrincipal/&lt;/code&gt; prefix followed by your tag key. This query groups costs by those tags to answer questions like: &lt;em&gt;“How much did the engineering team spend on Bedrock this month?”&lt;/em&gt; or &lt;em&gt;“What’s the total Bedrock cost for the chatbot project?”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;By project:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT
    tags['iamPrincipal/project'] AS project,
    line_item_usage_type,
    SUM(line_item_usage_amount) AS total_tokens,
    SUM(line_item_unblended_cost) AS total_cost
FROM your_cur_table_name
WHERE line_item_product_code in ('AmazonBedrock', 'AmazonBedrockService')
    AND billing_period = DATE_FORMAT(CURRENT_DATE, '%Y-%m')
    AND line_item_iam_principal IS NOT NULL
GROUP BY
    tags['iamPrincipal/project'],
    line_item_usage_type
ORDER BY total_cost DESC;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Example output:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;project&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;line_item_usage_type&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_tokens&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_cost&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;data-science&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.5Sonnet-cache-write-input-token-count&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;433.893&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1.789808625&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;data-science&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.6Sonnet-cache-read-input-token-count&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;5372.659&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1.77297747&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;engineering&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.5Sonnet-input-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;29.481&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0.0972873&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;engineering&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USW2-Claude4.5Sonnet-output-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;31.102&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0.513183&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Note:&lt;/strong&gt; This query returns results only if your IAM principals have been tagged with the relevant keys and those tags have been activated as cost allocation tags.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="query-3-dynamic-tag-discovery-using-unnest-for-unknown-tag-schemas"&gt;Query 3: Dynamic tag discovery using UNNEST for unknown tag schemas&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In large organizations, you may not know in advance which tags have been applied across all IAM principals. Different teams might use different tag keys, or new tags might be introduced over time. The following example shows how to dynamically explore using Athena’s UNNEST function.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This query discovers all IAM principal tags being used across your Bedrock workloads and shows cost allocation for each tag key-value pair:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;WITH iam_principal_costs AS (
    SELECT
        t.key AS tag_name,
        t.value AS tag_value,
        line_item_usage_type,
        line_item_unblended_cost
    FROM your_cur_table_name
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST(tags) AS t(key, value)
    WHERE line_item_product_code IN ('AmazonBedrock', 'AmazonBedrockService')
        AND line_item_iam_principal IS NOT NULL
        AND line_item_iam_principal != ''
        AND t.key LIKE 'iamPrincipal/%'
)
SELECT
    tag_name || ': ' || tag_value AS tags,
    line_item_usage_type,
    SUM(line_item_unblended_cost) AS total_cost
FROM iam_principal_costs
GROUP BY tag_name, tag_value, line_item_usage_type
ORDER BY total_cost DESC;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="real-world-use-case-multi-service-cost-comparison"&gt;Real-world use case: Multi-service cost comparison&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Consider a platform team running multiple AI-powered services. For example, the team might run a document summarization pipeline (DocProcessor) and a customer-facing chatbot (ChatApp). The team can assign each service to its own IAM role. With the query patterns from the preceding section, you can isolate how the spend for each service trends with the following query:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT
    line_item_iam_principal,
    line_item_usage_type,
    SUM(line_item_usage_amount) AS total_usage,
    SUM(line_item_unblended_cost) AS total_cost
FROM your_cur_table_name
WHERE line_item_product_code IN ('AmazonBedrock', 'AmazonBedrockService')
    AND billing_period = DATE_FORMAT(CURRENT_DATE, '%Y-%m')
    AND line_item_iam_principal IS NOT NULL
    AND (
        line_item_iam_principal LIKE '%DocProcessor%'
        OR line_item_iam_principal LIKE '%ChatApp%'
    )
GROUP BY
    line_item_iam_principal,
    line_item_usage_type
ORDER BY total_cost DESC;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Example output:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;line_item_iam_principal&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;line_item_usage_type&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_usage&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;total_cost&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/ChatApp/session-1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USE1-Claude4.6Sonnet-output-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4,800,000&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$72.00&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/ChatApp/session-1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USE1-Claude4.6Sonnet-input-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2,900,000&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$8.70&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/DocProcessor/batch-7&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USE1-NovaLite-output-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;6,100,000&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$1.46&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;arn:aws:sts::123456789012:assumed-role/DocProcessor/batch-7&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;USE1-NovaLite-input-tokens&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3,200,000&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;$0.19&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;From this output, the platform team can answer questions like:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Which application is the top contributor to this month’s Bedrock spend?&lt;/strong&gt; In this example, ChatApp accounts for over $80 using Claude 4.6 Sonnet, while DocProcessor costs under $5 using Nova Lite.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Could we reduce costs by using a different model for each workload?&lt;/strong&gt; DocProcessor is already on Nova Lite (appropriate for straightforward summarization tasks), but the team might evaluate whether ChatApp could handle some interactions with a lighter model to reduce the $72 output-token cost.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="cost-of-athena-queries"&gt;Cost of Athena queries&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You pay only for the queries that you run. You are charged based on the amount of data scanned by each query. The console displays this information after each query, and this information is also available in the Recent Queries tab in the Athena console.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Athena queries are billed at $5 per TB scanned (with a 10 MB minimum per query). Because our table automatically uses &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/athena/latest/ug/partition-projection.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;hive partition projection&lt;/a&gt; on billing_period, queries scoped to a single month only scan the parquet files in that month’s folder. Scans are typically well under 10 MB, which costs about $0.00005 per query (the 10 MB minimum).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To keep costs low, always include a &lt;code&gt;WHERE billing_period = ...&lt;/code&gt; filter and select only the columns you need rather than &lt;code&gt;SELECT *&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="the-cloud-intelligence-dashboards-framework"&gt;The Cloud Intelligence Dashboards framework&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/cudos-cid-kpi.html#foundational-cudos-dashboard" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;CUDOS dashboard&lt;/a&gt; is part of the open source &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/getting-started.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Cloud Intelligence Dashboards (CID) framework&lt;/a&gt;, which you can deploy in your AWS account using the provided infrastructure as code (IaC) templates. The framework helps you drive financial accountability and increase operational efficiency across your AWS organizations. The CUDOS dashboard provides detailed and actionable insights, supporting data-driven decisions for cost efficiency across your AWS infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="amazon-bedrock-cost-and-usage-insights-in-cudos"&gt;Amazon Bedrock cost and usage insights in CUDOS&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-solutions-library-samples/cloud-intelligence-dashboards-framework/blob/main/changes/CHANGELOG-cudos.md#cudos---580" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;CUDOS version 5.8&lt;/a&gt; introduces a comprehensive Amazon Bedrock section in the AI/ML tab, with full IAM principal cost attribution support. The dashboard provides:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Flexible grouping dimensions:&lt;/strong&gt; Group your Amazon Bedrock spend by IAM Principal, IAM Principal Tags (such as Project or Team), Model/Resource Group, Region, or any other cost taxonomy fields configured during dashboard deployment.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cost-per-million-tokens tracking:&lt;/strong&gt; A trend line overlaid on your spend chart showing how cost per million tokens evolves over time, helping you measure the impact of model selection changes or prompt optimization efforts, like caching for example.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following figure shows the Amazon Bedrock Summary section in the AI/ML tab of the CUDOS dashboard, grouped by IAM principal, with per-principal cost breakdown and cost-per-million-tokens trend.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-21078-3.png" alt="CUDOS dashboard AI/ML tab showing Amazon Bedrock spend grouped by IAM principal with a cost-per-million-tokens trend line" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;CUDOS dashboard showing Amazon Bedrock spend grouped by IAM principal&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Interactive drill-down filtering:&lt;/strong&gt; Choose any value in the top-level spend chart (such as a specific project, principal, or account) and every other visual automatically filters to that selection, letting you drill from a high-level overview into per-model and per-usage-type detail without navigating away from the dashboard.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Granular model and usage breakdown:&lt;/strong&gt; Additional visuals, filtered by the top-level chart, show spend per model, per usage type, and cost-per-million-tokens by model, so you can identify which models and token types are driving costs for a given team or project.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you switch the grouping to IAM Principal Tag Project and choose a specific project (in this example, “chatbot-v2”), all other visuals filter to show only that project’s spend. The visuals break down that spend by model, usage type, and unit cost trends.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-21078-4.png" alt="CUDOS dashboard showing Amazon Bedrock spend for the chatbot-v2 project broken down by model and usage type" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;CUDOS dashboard showing Amazon Bedrock spend grouped by IAM principal tag Project, filtered to the chatbot-v2 project&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With these visuals, you can quickly answer questions like “Which project is driving the most output token costs?”, “Is our chatbot team using cost-efficient models?”, or “How has our cost per million tokens changed since we switched from Opus to Sonnet?”, without writing any SQL.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="getting-started-with-cudos"&gt;Getting started with CUDOS&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started with CUDOS, you can explore the Bedrock section in an &lt;a href="https://cid.workshops.aws.dev/demo/?dashboard=cudos&amp;amp;sheet=AI/ML" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;interactive demo&lt;/a&gt; dashboard. Follow the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/deployment-in-global-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;deployment guide&lt;/a&gt; to set up CUDOS in your organization.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you’re already using CUDOS, follow the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/update-dashboards.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;update guidance&lt;/a&gt; to upgrade to version 5.8. You can also use the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/add-org-taxonomy.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;add organizational taxonomy&lt;/a&gt; to add IAM Principal data to your existing CUDOS dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;First, drop the Athena table and AWS Glue database (these are metadata, so no compute is running):&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Warning: Dropping the Athena table and Glue database will remove your ability to query CUR data. You will need to recreate these resources following the preceding section if you want to analyze billing data in the future.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws glue delete-table --region us-east-1 --database-name your_cur_table_name --name curexport
aws glue delete-database --region us-east-1 --name your_cur_table_name&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Second, if you no longer need the cost data itself, disable the export in the AWS Billing and Cost Management console under Data Exports and empty the S3 prefix it writes to. Note this is your raw billing history, so only delete it if you’re sure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Finally, clear out any Athena query results that have accumulated:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws s3 rm s3://&amp;lt;your-cur-bucket&amp;gt;/athena-results/ --recursive&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;There are no crawlers, AWS Lambda functions, or schedules to delete. Partition projection means the only ongoing cost is S3 storage for the CUR files themselves, which is typically pennies per month.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For cleaning up CUDOS, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/guidance/latest/cloud-intelligence-dashboards/dashboard-teardown.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;CUDOS dashboard teardown instructions&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion-and-next-steps"&gt;Conclusion and next steps&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This two-part series walked through a complete toolkit for understanding and managing Amazon Bedrock inference costs:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Part 1&lt;/strong&gt; introduced granular cost attribution: how Amazon Bedrock automatically captures the IAM principal behind every inference call, and how you can use cost allocation tags to aggregate spend by team, project, or tenant.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Part 2&lt;/strong&gt; (this post) showed you how to put that data to work: setting up CUR 2.0 with IAM principal data, querying cost patterns in Amazon Athena, and comparing spend across projects and principals to inform cost allocation decisions. It also introduces the CUDOS dashboard, which provides a comprehensive AI/ML sheet with similar insights for Bedrock.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Turn on CUR 2.0 with caller identity data in the AWS Billing console, connect it to Athena using the provided agent.md file, and run your first cost-by-principal query. Track Bedrock adoption across your organization with the CUDOS dashboard.&lt;/p&gt;
